HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE SANJEEV PRAKASH SHARMA
 HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE SANJEET PUROHIT
Order
11/09/2025
1. In all the present petitions, the challenge is to the order
passed under Section 164(2)(a) of the Companies Act, 2013 (for
short ‘the Act of 2013’), whereby the petitioners who were holding
the post of Directors, were disqualified on account of non-filing of
annual returns for the period and in such cases relating to the
period 2014, 2015 and 2016 and in some cases relating to period
2016, 2017 and 2018. The order was passed by the Registrar Of
Companies (for short ‘ROC’), and the same was challenged before
this Court in writ petition.

[2025:RJ-JP:37072-DB] (156 of 158) [CW-9599/2019]
2. The Division Bench of this Court vide order dated
31.05.2019, in the lead case of Smt. Richa Sharma Vs. Union
of India, passed the following order:-
“Issue notice to respondents, returnable by four
weeks.
In the meanwhile, the respondents are
directed tor reactivate the Director Identification
Number (DIN), of the petitioner and the digital
signature certificate to enable the petitioner to act
in the capacity of Director and file the necessary
annual returns so also to discharge statutory
obligations of all other companies wherein the
petitioner is Director.
Connect with D.B. Civil Writ Petition
No.7997/2019”
3. A similar order has been passed in most of the cases which
are tagged along with these files. In few of the cases, interim
order was not passed, however, the fact remains that after
passing of the order in the year 2019, several of them have
continued to perform their work as Directors in terms of the
interim order as above.
4. Learned Additional Solicitor General has invited our attention
to the provisions of Section 164(2)(a)(b) of the Act of 2013, to
submit that the period of disqualification at the best can be
continued for five years and after five years, the position of the
concerned Directors would be restored. He further submits that
the period is to be counted from the date when the initial order
was passed.

[2025:RJ-JP:37072-DB] (157 of 158) [CW-9599/2019]
5. It would be apposite to quote the provisions of Section
164(2)(a)(b) of the Act of 2013:-
“(2) No person who is or has been a director of a
company which-
(a) has not filed financial statements or annual
returns for any continuous period of three financial
years; or
(b) has failed to repay the deposits accepted by it
or pay interest thereon or to redeem any
debentures on the due date or pay interest due
thereon or pay any dividend declared and such
failure to pay or redeem continues for one year or
more,
shall be eligible to be re-appointed as a director of
that company or appointed in other company for a
period of five years from the date on which the said
company fails to do so.
[Provided that where a person is appointed as a
director of a company which is in default of clause
(a) or clause (b), he shall not incur the
disqualification for a period of six months from the
date of his appointment”
6. The learned counsels appearing for the writ petitioners,
however, submit that the cases should be decided on merits as on
account of the disqualification orders passed, consequential action
may be taken against the concerned petitioners, who were holding
the post of Directors.
7. Learned Additional Solicitor General has however submitted
that no consequential action has been taken against the
petitioners in view of the interim order passed by this Court and
now by efflux of time since the Directors would be restored to
their original post, the issue has become purely academic.

[2025:RJ-JP:37072-DB] (158 of 158) [CW-9599/2019]
8. We have considered the submissions made on behalf of both
the sides. We notice that so far as the issue with regard to the
power of declaring the Directors disqualified in terms of Section
164(2)(a)(b) of the Act of 2013, the basis of non-filing of annual
returns for the previous years have been examined by various
High Courts (refer to Bhagwan Das Dhananjaya Das Vs. Union
of India 2018 SCC OnLine Mad 13820; Gaurang Balvantal
Shah vs Union of India 2018 SCC OnLine Guj 4866;
Yashodhara Shroff vs Union of India 2019 SCC OnLine Kar
682).
9. In the present case, we find that more than five years have
lapsed and we have also noticed that no consequential action has
been taken at the level of the ROC, as against the Directors during
this intervening period. In view of provisions of Section 164(2)(b)
of the Act of 2013 and Proviso thereto, we find that the petitioners
would be restored to the position of Director.
10. We are therefore inclined to accept the contentions raised by
the Additional Solicitor General that by efflux of time, the inter se
dispute has become purely academic and the writ petitions have
been rendered otiose. We leave the question of law open to be
examined in appropriate case if so arises. Accordingly, we dispose
of all the writ petitions making our interim order absolute and rule
of the Court.
11. Pending application(s), if any, stand(s) disposed of.
12. A copy of this order be placed in each connected file.
(SANJEET PUROHIT),J (SANJEEV PRAKASH SHARMA),J
AVINASH GULERIA /48-510
